# Verification register

This repository was built by research agents working from public sources. Where a fact could not be confirmed against a primary source it was **flagged rather than invented** — that discipline is what makes the rest of the corpus trustworthy, but it means an agent using this knowledge base must read the flags.

## How to use this file

1. Before relying on any number, clause reference, price or specification for a decision with real consequences, check whether its file appears below.
2. Treat `confidence: low` and `status: needs-verification` as **do not act on this without checking**.
3. Treat `confidence: medium` as **usable for orientation, verify before commitment**.
4. Every file carries its own `## Open questions` section; those are reproduced in full in the second half of this document.

> ⚠️ Three categories recur across the whole corpus and should be assumed unverified everywhere: **prices and rates** (almost no Namibian or South African price list is publicly retrievable), **paywalled standards text** (SANS clause values in particular), and **labour output rates** (trade convention, not published data).

### [`01_architecture/00_overview.md`](../01_architecture/00_overview.md) — Architecture domain overview

- The exact prescribed period of practical training and the form of the prescribed examination under **[NA]** Architects' and Quantity Surveyors' Act s.11(2) are not published on the NCAQS public website; they must be obtained from the Council directly.
- Whether Namibia has formally adopted SANS 10400 as a national building code, or whether building control remains entirely at local-authority level, could not be confirmed from a primary source.

### [`01_architecture/01_pathway-to-registration.md`](../01_architecture/01_pathway-to-registration.md) — Pathway to registration as an architect

- **[NA]** The prescribed period of practical training, the form and content of the prescribed examination, and current NCAQS fee levels under s.7 are not published online; confirm directly with the Council.
- **[NA]** The current penalty under Act s.13 (the 1979 text states "one thousand rand") has almost certainly been revised; not verified.
- **[ZA]** Current SACAP application, candidacy and annual registration fees, and the current PPE fee, were not obtainable from a primary source at the date of writing. The R390 / R2,377 figures are 2023 quotes.
- Per-division ARE 5.0 examination fees were not confirmed from NCARB's own fee page (404 at time of access).
- Whether the SACAP PPE is now held twice yearly as standard, or whether 2023 was exceptional, is unresolved between the PPE information sheet (annual) and the 2023/24 annual report (two sittings).

### [`01_architecture/02_accredited-schools-and-curricula.md`](../01_architecture/02_accredited-schools-and-curricula.md) — Accredited architecture schools and model curriculum

- Exact NQF levels and credit totals per programme are not published consistently across SA institutions; the levels shown are inferred from the SACAP competency document's category-to-NQF mapping.
- Accreditation expiry dates for each ALS were not obtainable; SACAP publishes visit reports but not a consolidated validity table. Re-verify before relying on a specific programme's status.
- CPUT and DUT programme names have changed with the phasing out of the National Diploma/BTech structure; the SACAP list may lag the institutions' current offerings. Marked `confidence: medium` for that reason.
- The credit-share percentages in the model curriculum are a distillation of common practice, not a SACAP-prescribed weighting.

### [`01_architecture/03_design-fundamentals.md`](../01_architecture/03_design-fundamentals.md) — Design fundamentals — parti, site, order and dimension

- Room-area and workstation guides in section 3 are practice norms distilled from the Metric Handbook / Neufert tradition rather than statutory minima; only the SANS-cited figures are legally binding.
- SANS 10400-T escape-route **widths** (clause 4.21) and the occupant-load table could not be read from the archived PDF excerpt; consult the full standard before sizing corridors on fire grounds.
- **[NA]** Equivalent Namibian dimensional minima (Municipality of Windhoek Building Regulations, G.N. 57 of 1969) were not extracted clause-by-clause; do not assume SANS numbers apply automatically in Namibia.
- Brick nominal dimensions vary by manufacturer; verify against the supplier's data before setting out.

### [`01_architecture/04_drawing-and-documentation.md`](../01_architecture/04_drawing-and-documentation.md) — Drawing types, scales, conventions and the construction set

- SANS 10143 is a 1980 edition and was accessed only via third-party transcriptions, not from SABS directly; clause numbers are therefore **not** cited in this file and the scale/line-weight tables should be treated as practice convention corroborated by the standard rather than verbatim quotation. Marked `confidence: medium` for that reason.
- The line-weight table is industry practice; SANS 10143 specifies graduated weights but the specific millimetre values above are conventional, not quoted from the standard.
- **[NA]** The City of Windhoek does not publish required drawing scales or copy counts for *building plan* (as distinct from urban planning) submissions; confirm with Building Control before issue.
- Whether **[NA]** requires plans to be submitted by a registered architectural professional (as opposed to merely recording their registration number) is not stated on the submission form; note that urban planning applications *must* be submitted by a registered town planner.

### [`01_architecture/05_building-science.md`](../01_architecture/05_building-science.md) — Building science — thermal, moisture, ventilation, light, sound, sun

- **All solar altitudes, azimuths, overhang projections and zenith-passage dates in §5 are computed by the standard declination formulae, not taken from a published almanac.** They are accurate to roughly ±0,5° / ±2 days and are adequate for shading design, but should be confirmed against an ephemeris or sun-path tool for critical work.
- Eenhana's Köppen classification is not stated in the cited source; "BSh/Aw" is inferred from the temperature and rainfall pattern and is **not verified**.
- Time-lag and decrement-factor values in §1 are conventional practice figures, not drawn from a cited standard.
- **[NA]** No Namibian statutory thermal performance requirement was found. Whether SANS 10400-XA has been formally adopted in Namibia is unresolved.
- SANS 10400-XA Table 4 fenestration U-value and SHGC values were not extracted line-by-line; consult the standard for compliance calculations.
- Reverberation-time targets are drawn from general acoustic practice; ANSI S12.60 and equivalent SANS provisions should be checked for regulated building types.

### [`01_architecture/06_structures-for-architects.md`](../01_architecture/06_structures-for-architects.md) — Structures for architects — load paths and preliminary sizing

- The SANS 10160-2 imposed-load table was read from a **third-party transcription**, not from SABS. Values (including the unusual 2,3 kPa for general offices) must be checked against the published standard before use. This is why the file is marked `status: draft`, `confidence: medium`.
- SANS 10160-3 wind pressure coefficients, terrain categories and the map of fundamental basic wind speeds were not obtained; only the three basic wind speeds (36/40/44 m/s) and the SANS 10400-K minimum pressures are cited.
- SANS 10400-K deemed-to-satisfy tables (wall height/length/thickness matrices) were not read directly; the Category 1 limits quoted come from a peer-reviewed review paper, not the standard itself.
- Dead-load, bearing-pressure, column-sizing and movement-joint figures are conventional engineering practice values and are **not** cited to a standard. Treat as order-of-magnitude only.
- **[NA]** Namibia's applicable loading code (whether SANS 10160 is adopted, or another regime applies) was not established.

### [`01_architecture/07_professional-practice.md`](../01_architecture/07_professional-practice.md) — Professional practice — services, fees, contracts and liability

- The current PROCSA edition is ambiguous: ACEN promotes **Edition 4.0 (October 2017)**, while an ASAQS circular refers to **Edition 5**. Confirm the current edition before issuing an agreement.
- The SAIA client guide and the gazetted SACAP guideline give **different stage percentages**; the SAIA figures as fetched also do not sum cleanly. Use the gazette.
- Whether a **Board Notice later than 672 of 2024** has updated the SACAP fee guideline for 2025/2026 was not established; check the current gazette before quoting rates.
- **[NA]** No Namibian gazetted fee guideline was located. Namibian practice appears to reference the SACAP guideline and PROCSA; this should be confirmed with NCAQS and the NIA.
- JBCC edition: the contract data fetched corresponds to Edition 6.2 (May 2018) clause numbering; confirm against the edition actually incorporated in any given contract, as clause numbers move between editions.
- The exact interim payment certificate **issue** interval is set in the contract data, not fixed by the agreement; monthly is conventional but not automatic.

### [`01_architecture/08_reading-list-and-resources.md`](../01_architecture/08_reading-list-and-resources.md) — Reading list, standards and open resources for architects

- **Editions and ISBNs for the majority of titles were not individually verified.** Only Metric Handbook (7th, 2021, 9781003052586), Architecture: Form, Space, and Order (5th, 9781119853374), Fundamentals of Building Construction (7th, 9781119446194), Heating Cooling Lighting (5th, 9781119585749) and Sun Wind and Light (3rd, 9780470945780) were confirmed against publisher pages. Neufert, Ching's *Building Construction Illustrated*, *Architectural Graphic Standards* and the AIA *Handbook* are cited without a verified current edition (Wiley's pages returned 403/404 at time of access).
- *The Architect's Handbook of Professional Practice* is listed at 15th ed. (ISBN 9781118308820) from a retail listing; a 16th edition may exist.
- The podcast, journal and course lists are curated recommendations, not verified against publisher/producer pages; availability changes.
- No Namibia-specific architectural practice manual or textbook was located. If one exists (e.g. published by the NIA), it should be added.
- Whether SANS standards are available free of charge to South African users (they were at some periods for the SANS 10400 series) could not be confirmed; the archived copies linked in other files in this domain are third-party scans, not official distributions.

### [`02_building_construction/00_overview.md`](../02_building_construction/00_overview.md) — Building construction competence map

- Current Namibian building control practice at regional/local authority level in Ohangwena (plan approval, inspections) is not documented in an accessible primary source and should be confirmed with the Ohangwena Regional Council and the relevant town council before relying on any assumption of SANS-based approval.

### [`02_building_construction/01_builder-competency-roadmap.md`](../02_building_construction/01_builder-competency-roadmap.md) — Builder competency roadmap — trade to contractor

- The exact duration in months of each NTA National Vocational Certificate level for Bricklaying and Plastering, and the current NTA trade-test entry requirements (years of experience for RPL candidates), were not obtainable from a primary NTA source and are marked `needs-verification`.
- Whether cidb tender value ranges have been amended since the July 2021 Best Practice Guideline A6 could not be confirmed from a primary cidb regulation.
- Current NHBRC home builder registration and home enrolment fees were not published on an accessible NHBRC page.
- The current Affirmative Action (Employment) Act employee-number threshold for a "relevant employer" in Namibia should be confirmed with the Employment Equity Commission.

### [`02_building_construction/02_site-establishment-and-setting-out.md`](../02_building_construction/02_site-establishment-and-setting-out.md) — Site establishment and setting out

- Numerical setting-out tolerances (position, overall dimension, diagonal difference, trench width, foundation level) could not be sourced from SANS or another primary standard and are given as trade practice; they are marked `needs-verification` for anyone relying on them contractually.
- Ohangwena Regional Council / Okongo Town Council requirements for building plan approval, site inspections and boundary certification are not documented in an accessible online source.

### [`02_building_construction/03_groundworks-and-foundations.md`](../02_building_construction/03_groundworks-and-foundations.md) — Groundworks and foundations

- Presumed allowable bearing pressures are given as indicative engineering practice; they are **not** sourced from a South African primary standard and must be verified against SANS 10160-5 / a geotechnical report before design use.
- Nominal concrete cover values are indicative; SANS 10100-2 Table 7 sets cover by exposure condition and concrete grade and was not obtainable in full.
- Minimum founding depth in deep aeolian sand at Okongo is given as trade practice (600 mm); no Namibian standard specifying founding depth was located.

### [`02_building_construction/04_concrete-technology.md`](../02_building_construction/04_concrete-technology.md) — Concrete technology for builders

- The 1,0 kg/m²/h evaporation-rate threshold for mandatory hot-weather precautions is international practice (ACI-derived) and was not found in the Cement & Concrete SA leaflets; treat as `needs-verification` for southern African use.
- Numerical maximum water:cement ratios by exposure class (as in SANS 10100-2) were not obtainable from a primary source; the w/c figures given are working guides derived from the published cement contents.
- The 7-day / 28-day strength ratio quoted is general engineering practice, not a sourced SANS figure.

### [`02_building_construction/05_masonry-and-brickwork.md`](../02_building_construction/05_masonry-and-brickwork.md) — Masonry and brickwork

- SANS 2001-CM1:2012 could not be obtained in full; the plumb/level/line tolerances given are trade practice and need verification against the standard's permissible deviations clause.
- The "5 crimp ties per m²" figure for collar-jointed walls is taken from a Clay Brick Technical Guide figure caption rather than from the SANS clause text.
- Namibian availability and pricing of specific unit sizes at Ondangwa/Oshakati depots is not documented here; confirm locally before designing to a coursing dimension.

### [`02_building_construction/06_roofing-and-carpentry.md`](../02_building_construction/06_roofing-and-carpentry.md) — Roofing and carpentry

- Numerical CCA **retention values (kg/m³) per hazard class** under SANS 10005 / SANS 457 were not published in the SAWPA material consulted and are marked `needs-verification`.
- IBR **load–span tables by gauge** are described by manufacturers as guidance only and were not obtainable; purlin spacing for IBR must be taken from the specific manufacturer's current load–span table or from an engineer.
- Rafter and purlin size/span tables for cut roofs in SA pine were not sourced from SANS 10082 and the sizes given are indicative trade practice.
- Wind loading for the Ohangwena region (SANS 10160-3 basic wind speed) was not verified; truss and anchor design must use the correct regional wind speed.

### [`02_building_construction/07_finishes-plaster-screed-paint.md`](../02_building_construction/07_finishes-plaster-screed-paint.md) — Finishes — plaster, screed, tiling and paint

- Minimum drying time for new plaster before painting, and alkali-resistant primer requirements, are given as general trade practice; they were not obtainable from an accessible South African paint manufacturer's technical data sheet and are marked `needs-verification`.
- Spreading rates (m²/ℓ) for specific SA paint systems are not given here because no primary source was available.
- SANS 10107 was not obtainable in full; the tiling values quoted come from a manufacturer's general specification that itself references SANS 10107.

### [`02_building_construction/08_waterproofing-and-damp.md`](../02_building_construction/08_waterproofing-and-damp.md) — Waterproofing and damp

- Minimum falls for flat roofs (as opposed to gutters) are not stated numerically in the SANS 10021 text retrieved; the 1:80 / 1:60 figures commonly used in practice are given in `07_finishes-plaster-screed-paint.md` as trade practice and are `needs-verification`.
- Standard membrane thicknesses for torch-on systems (commonly quoted as 3 mm or 4 mm APP/SBS) were not found in the SANS 10021 text and should be taken from the specific manufacturer's specification.
- Typical guarantee periods offered by South African waterproofing manufacturers were not verified from a primary source.
- The shower waterproofing detail dimensions given (upstand heights, 300 mm past the threshold) are trade practice; the TAL specification should be consulted for the project-specific figures.

### [`02_building_construction/09_plumbing-and-drainage.md`](../02_building_construction/09_plumbing-and-drainage.md) — Plumbing and drainage

- SANS 10252-2 Table 17 (maximum spacing of access points) is a matrix whose row/column pairing could not be reliably reconstructed from the plain-text scan; the range 12–90 m is correct but the specific pairing must be read from the standard.
- Indicative domestic pipe sizes (25/22/15 mm) are trade practice, not a SANS table; SANS 10252-1 requires sizing by calculation from the design flow rate and design flow pressure.
- The edition of SANS 10252-2 available in full text is the 1993 (SABS 0252-2) edition; check for a current edition before relying on it contractually.
- Namibian local-authority requirements for on-site sanitation approval in Ohangwena were not obtainable.

### [`02_building_construction/10_electrical-fundamentals.md`](../02_building_construction/10_electrical-fundamentals.md) — Electrical fundamentals for builders

- Cable size / breaker pairings for domestic circuits are common practice, not a SANS table; SANS 10142-1 requires selection from tables 6.2–6.9 with correction factors. `needs-verification`.
- The full-text SANS 10142-1 available is an older edition (Ed 1.5, Amdt 5 / 2006 base) — clause numbering and some requirements have changed in the 2017 (Ed 2) and 2024 (Ed 3.2) editions. Verify clause numbers against the current edition.
- Chasing geometry rules are trade practice; no SANS clause was located that specifies maximum chase depth in masonry.
- **[NA]** Namibian certificate-of-compliance requirements, the registration route for electricians, and whether SANS 10142-1 is legally incorporated could not be confirmed from a primary Namibian source.
- Peak sun hours (5,0–5,5) for Okongo is an engineering estimate, not a sourced figure; confirm against a solar resource dataset for the exact site.

### [`02_building_construction/11_estimating-and-measurement.md`](../02_building_construction/11_estimating-and-measurement.md) — Estimating and measurement

- The **Standard System of Measuring Building Work is now in its 7th edition (2015)**; the freely available full texts are the 1999 6th edition and the Small or Simple Buildings edition. Measurement rules quoted here should be checked against the 7th edition for contractual use.
- Labour constants (hours per unit) for southern African trades could not be sourced from a published authority and are deliberately **not** tabulated here; the file gives the method for deriving them instead.
- Waste factors other than mortar (20–50 %), plaster (20 %) and screed (10 %) are conventional trade allowances and are `needs-verification`.
- JBCC retention percentages, payment periods and guarantee amounts are contract-data items in a paid document and were not verifiable from a free source.

### [`02_building_construction/12_programme-and-site-management.md`](../02_building_construction/12_programme-and-site-management.md) — Programme and site management

- Activity durations and lead times in this file are planning estimates based on typical small-contractor practice, **not** sourced values. They are marked `needs-verification` and should be replaced with the builder's own recorded data.
- Ohangwena rainy-season dates are given as an approximation; confirm against Namibia Meteorological Service records for Okongo.
- Namibian equivalents of the ZA health and safety file requirements and any local-authority inspection stages were not obtainable.

### [`02_building_construction/13_quality-defects-and-tolerances.md`](../02_building_construction/13_quality-defects-and-tolerances.md) — Quality, defects and permissible deviations

- Masonry verticality, level, straightness and joint-thickness tolerances are trade practice; **SANS 2001-CM1:2012** contains the permissible deviations for masonry walling and could not be obtained in full. All values marked **(T)** in §5 need verification against it.
- Tile lippage and tiled-surface flatness tolerances are trade practice; SANS 10107 could not be obtained in full.
- Setting-out tolerances are trade practice; no South African standard specifying them was located.
- Cement & Concrete SA notes that neither the SABS specifications nor the National Building Regulations set measurable requirements for hardened plaster quality — so acceptance criteria for plaster hardness must be agreed in the specification before work starts.

### [`02_building_construction/14_health-and-safety.md`](../02_building_construction/14_health-and-safety.md) — Construction health and safety

- **[ZA]** Draft Construction Regulations, 2024 have been published for public comment; the 2014 Regulations remain the operative instrument at the date of this file but the position should be re-checked before relying on the permit thresholds.
- Whether the Chief Inspector's exemption referred to in the notes to Construction Regulation 3 is currently in force, and its terms, was not verified.
- **[NA]** Namibian first aid box contents (Annexure G(3)(a)) and first aid attendant qualification requirements were not extracted in detail.
- **[NA]** Namibia has no construction-specific regulations equivalent to the ZA Construction Regulations 2014 (no construction work permit, no statutory health and safety file, no mandatory construction manager appointment); the general GN 156/1997 regime applies. Confirm whether any sector-specific instrument has since been made.

### [`03_codes_standards/00_overview.md`](../03_codes_standards/00_overview.md) — Building codes and standards in South Africa and Namibia — how the regulatory stack works

- Whether any Namibian local authority has *formally* incorporated the SANS 10400 series by reference under LAA s 94B, and if so which parts and which editions. The Windhoek regulations as published reference pre-1990 SABS specifications (SABS 5, 61, 538, 563, 629), not SANS 10400. Requires confirmation from each council's gazetted regulations.
- The current status of the NSI national building code project begun in 2012 — whether any Namibian standard for building design has since been declared under Standards Act s 20, and whether any has been declared compulsory under s 20(6).
- Whether Namibian courts or councils have treated SANS 10400 as evidence of the professional standard of care in construction disputes. No public case law located.

### [`03_codes_standards/01_south-africa-nbr-act.md`](../03_codes_standards/01_south-africa-nbr-act.md) — South Africa — National Building Regulations and Building Standards Act 103 of 1977

- The exact current wording of Regulation A2 (plans and particulars) and A23 in the latest amendment — the consolidated municipal reprint used here dates from 2008 and the regulations have been amended since (notably the insertion of Part XA). Confirm against the latest Government Gazette or SANS 10400-A:2025 (Ed. 4.01).
- Whether the s 24 general penalty of R4 000 has been adjusted by any subsequent amendment or by the Adjustment of Fines Act. Not verified from a public source.
- The prescribed qualifications for a building control officer under s 5 — set by regulation; the specific regulation and qualification list was not verified from a public source.
- Whether Part U (Refuse disposal) and Part E (Demolition work) have corresponding published SANS 10400 parts. The SABS webstore listing accessed 2026-08-25 shows no SANS 10400-E or SANS 10400-U; this suggests those regulations have no separate deemed-to-satisfy standard, but that should be confirmed with SABS.

### [`03_codes_standards/02_sans-10400-parts-register.md`](../03_codes_standards/02_sans-10400-parts-register.md) — SANS 10400 parts register — A to XA

- **Every value above tagged to a 2010–2012 edition needs re-verification against the current edition** listed in the price table. Parts A, B, F, G, H, J, K, M, N, O, S and XA have all been re-issued since those archived editions.
- Whether SANS 10400-E (Demolition work) and SANS 10400-U (Refuse disposal) exist at all. They do not appear in the SABS catalogue search performed on 2026-08-25. Confirm directly with SABS Standards Sales.
- Part D balustrade heights and swimming pool barrier requirements (Ed. 4.01, 2022) — not verified from a public source.
- Part H strip footing tables and the trigger thresholds for mandatory rational design of foundations (Ed. 3.01, 2026) — not verified from a public source.
- Part P clause 4.11 sanitary fixture provision tables — not verified from a public source.
- Part W hydrant spacing, flow rate and static storage requirements — not verified from a public source.
- Part K wall panel tables 1–7 — deliberately not reproduced (copyright); the worked-example limiting dimensions quoted are from the 2011 edition only.
- Whether SABS offers a discounted "SANS 10400 complete set" bundle price below the sum of the individual parts. Not verified.

### [`03_codes_standards/03_key-sans-standards-register.md`](../03_codes_standards/03_key-sans-standards-register.md) — Key SANS standards referenced on site — register by discipline

- Current editions of **SANS 542, 613, 878, 10021, 10082, 10254, 204** and the **SANS 1200 series** — none returned in the SABS catalogue search on 2026-08-25. They may be current but not indexed by that search, or superseded/withdrawn. Confirm with SABS Standards Sales (sales@sabs.co.za).
- Whether **SANS 204** has been withdrawn following SANS 10400-XA:2021 and :2026. Not verified.
- Whether the **SANS 1200 series** is formally withdrawn or merely deprecated in favour of SANS 2001. Not verified.
- The exact hazard-class-to-application mapping in the current **SANS 1288:2020** — the H2/H3/H4/H5/H6 assignments quoted here are from general industry practice, not from the purchased standard.
- The full list of published **SANS 2001** parts and their current editions. Only CM1, CM2 and CT2 were verified.
- Whether the Namibian **Cement Regulations GN 259** adopt SANS 50197-1 verbatim or set a distinct Namibian specification. Not verified — obtain the Government Notice from the NSI.

### [`03_codes_standards/04_namibia-building-regulations.md`](../03_codes_standards/04_namibia-building-regulations.md) — Namibia — how building control actually works

- **Does Okongo Village Council have gazetted building regulations under LAA s 94(1)(ag)?** If so, what Government Notice number, and what standard publications does it incorporate under s 94B? Not verified from any public source — must be obtained from the Village Council or the Ministry of Urban and Rural Development.
- **The surveyed boundary of the proclaimed Okongo Village Council area**, and the Government Notice that proclaimed it in 2015. Not located.
- **Which recognised Traditional Authority has jurisdiction over the Okongo area** for the purposes of customary land right allocation. Traditional and constituency boundaries do not coincide and this must be confirmed locally with the Ohangwena Communal Land Board.
- Whether **any** Namibian local authority has formally incorporated the SANS 10400 series by reference under s 94B, and which editions. The Windhoek regulations as published reference only pre-1990 SABS specifications.
- The **current gazetted building plan fee tariff** for Windhoek and for Okongo. The N$150–N$1 400 figures quoted are from the published consolidation and are almost certainly superseded.
- Whether the Windhoek regulations have been amended since GN 88 of 2002, and whether a metricated consolidation exists.
- Whether **regional councils** administering declared settlement areas exercise any building plan approval function, and under what power. The Regional Councils Act 22 of 1992 was not obtained in full.
- The current status of the **NSI national building code / Eurocode adoption project** begun in 2012.
- Whether the Communal Land Reform Act regulations impose any requirement or restriction on the *type* of structure that may be erected under a customary land right (beyond fencing under s 18/s 28). Not verified.

### [`03_codes_standards/05_namibia-approvals-and-institutions.md`](../03_codes_standards/05_namibia-approvals-and-institutions.md) — Namibia — approvals, permits and the institutions that issue them

- **NCAQS registration categories, reserved work and fees** under Act 13 of 1979 — not verified. Obtain from NCAQS directly.
- **Whether the Engineering Council of Namibia is currently constituted and operating**, following the reported dissolution dispute. Not verified.
- **The ECN's Schedule II Parts A and B** list of reserved engineering disciplines — not verified.
- **The content of GN 156 of 1997** (health and safety regulations) as it applies to construction sites — not verified. This is a significant gap for any Namibian project HSE plan.
- **The Ministry of Works and Transport's statutory role**, and whether State buildings are exempt from local authority building control in Namibia — not verified.
- **ECB licence conditions** relevant to a customer's own installation, and the exact electrical certification a distributor requires before energising a new connection — not verified.
- **NORED's published service-area map** and confirmation that Okongo falls within it — inferred from NORED references to Ohangwena Region, not confirmed for Okongo specifically.
- **Whether an ECC is required for a borehole for a single institutional building** (as opposed to "industrial/commercial purposes" under listed activity 8.1) — the threshold is not stated in the listed activities and must be confirmed with the Environmental Commissioner.
- **The Water Resources Management Act 11 of 2013 borehole drilling and abstraction permit thresholds** — not verified.

### [`03_codes_standards/06_energy-and-sustainability-requirements.md`](../03_codes_standards/06_energy-and-sustainability-requirements.md) — Energy and sustainability — SANS 10400-XA and SANS 204 in practice

- **All numeric values from the 2021 edition are medium confidence and must be re-verified against SANS 10400-XA:2026 (Ed. 3.00).** In particular: the Table 8 roof R-value per zone, the Table 6 and Table 7 wall values, and the Table 4 fenestration U-value/SHGC bands.
- **What changed between XA:2021 (Ed. 2) and XA:2026 (Ed. 3)** — not verified at all. Ed. 3 was published within the last year and its scope of change is unknown.
- **The names of energy zones 1–7 and 5H** in the 2021/2026 system. The 2021 edition appears to identify zones by number and by the Table C.1 town list rather than by descriptive names. Not verified.
- **Whether SANS 204 is still current or has been withdrawn.** Not verified.
- **The current SANS 10252-1:2018 table numbers** for the annual average hot water heating requirement calculation (the 2011 XA cited tables 2 and 5 of the 2004 edition).
- **Hot water pipe insulation thickness requirements** — the specific values were not verified from a public source.
- **Whether any Namibian local authority or the NSI has adopted any building energy requirement.** No evidence found either way.

### [`03_codes_standards/07_structural-loading-and-design-basis.md`](../03_codes_standards/07_structural-loading-and-design-basis.md) — Structural loading and design basis — limit states, wind, seismic, soils and the rational design

- **The site class movement ranges** (mm of expected total heave, collapse or settlement) defining classes R, H/H1/H2/H3, C/C1/C2, S/S1/S2 and P in Table 2.1 of the SAICE/IStructE *Code of Practice for Foundations and Superstructures*. **Not verified from any public source** — this is the most significant unverified item in this file. Obtain the SAICE code and the NHBRC Home Building Manual.
- **The NHBRC Home Building Manual foundation solutions** mapped against each site class. Not verified; the manual is published by the NHBRC but a working download URL was not located.
- **SANS 10160-1 partial factors, ψ-values and combination expressions** — not verified.
- **SANS 10160-2 imposed load values and barrier line loads** — not verified.
- **SANS 10160-3 basic wind speed map, terrain multipliers and pressure coefficients** — not verified.
- **SANS 10160-4 seismic hazard zonation and zone factors**, and the threshold below which seismic design may be omitted — not verified.
- **Whether a Namibian wind loading source exists** (a national basic wind speed map or an ECN/NSI guidance note). None located. This is a real gap for Namibian design.
- **SANS 10400-H trigger thresholds** at which deemed-to-satisfy foundations become unavailable and a rational design is mandatory — not verified.
- Whether SANS 10400-B:2025 (Ed. 4.01) changed any of the design life, wind pressure or tolerance values quoted here from Edition 3.

### [`03_codes_standards/08_fire-safety-requirements.md`](../03_codes_standards/08_fire-safety-requirements.md) — Fire safety — SANS 10400-T and SANS 10400-W in practice

- **All values above are from Edition 4 (2020). The current edition is SANS 10400-T:2024 (Ed. 5.01)** and its changes are unverified. Re-verify travel distances, Table 4, Table 18 and the category 1 provisions.
- **Table 1 and Table 2** (fire resistance of external walls, and boundary distances by fire load) — the numeric contents were not extracted and are not verified.
- **Table 9** (width of escape routes by population) — not verified.
- **Table 10** (fire detection requirements by occupancy; and the portable extinguisher table) — not verified.
- **Table 5** (emergency power supply duration) — not verified.
- **Clause 4.58** requirements for H5 guest houses and B&Bs — not verified.
- **SANS 10400-W** hydrant spacing, flow, pressure and static storage requirements — not verified. Local fire department requirements typically exceed the standard and must be obtained separately.
- **Clause 4.55** (presumed fire resistance of building materials and components) — the tables giving presumed ratings for common masonry, concrete and plasterboard constructions were not extracted. These are the tables a designer actually uses to demonstrate a 30, 60, 90 or 120 minute rating without testing.
- **[NA]** What fire requirements Namibian local authorities apply. The City of Windhoek names "Fire" as one of four plan-review heads but publishes no fire standard. Obtain the fire brigade's requirements directly.

### [`03_codes_standards/09_compliance-checklists.md`](../03_codes_standards/09_compliance-checklists.md) — Compliance checklists — residential plan submission, inspection stages and project document register

- **The City of Windhoek Building Plan Submission Check List (.xls)** was identified but its contents were not extracted (spreadsheet format). Download and reconcile against section B.3.
- **[ZA]** The **statutory inspection stages** are not specified in the Act or the NBR — they are set by each municipality's building by-law. The stage list in section C is compiled from general practice and must be verified against the specific municipality's by-law.
- **[NA]** The specific mandatory inspection stages a Council-authorised agent calls in Windhoek, and in Okongo, were not verified.
- **[ZA]** Whether a plumbing certificate of compliance is a national requirement or a municipal one — not verified; Regulation A18 controls the trade of plumbing but the certificate requirement appears to be municipal.
- **DPC height above finished ground level** — the commonly-cited 150 mm was not verified against the current SANS 10400-K/J and is flagged accordingly.
- **[NA]** Whether Okongo Village Council has a published submission checklist or inspection regime — unverified.
- Current plan scrutiny **fee tariffs** for any specific authority in either country.

### [`03_codes_standards/10_how-to-obtain-standards.md`](../03_codes_standards/10_how-to-obtain-standards.md) — How to legally obtain building standards, Acts and regulations

- **SABS subscription and collection pricing and terms** — not published on the store and not verified. Request from SABS Standards Sales.
- **The SABS "CLAIM TOKEN" and "CREDIT RECHARGE" mechanisms** — observed on the webstore but their commercial terms were not verified.
- **Whether SABS offers a discounted bundle price for the complete SANS 10400 set** — not verified; the ≈R16 400 figure is the simple sum of individual part prices.
- **Whether the SANS single-user PDF licence permits printing** and how many devices it may be opened on — not verified.
- **Whether SABS or the NSI operate a physical reading room** open to the public — not verified. The LAA s 94B(4) right in Namibia is confirmed; there is no known South African equivalent.
- **Current SABS Standards Sales email address and telephone number** — the SABS contact page could not be retrieved (TLS/robots restriction). Use the Help section of the webstore.
- **Whether Namibian university libraries (NUST, UNAM) hold SANS subscriptions** accessible to non-students — not verified, but worth checking for anyone working in Namibia.
- **Prices are ZAR and NAD as at 2026-08-25** and will change. Re-check before budgeting.

- Published comparative strength data for Domino loose tenons versus integral mortise and tenon is contested; no peer-reviewed source was located.
- Dovetail slope conventions (1:6 / 1:8) are traditional practice rather than a sourced standard.

- Extraction figures are converted from a US cfm reference; verify against the machine manufacturer's stated extraction port volume and static pressure requirement before sizing a system.

- Open and clamp times in the adhesive table are typical manufacturer values, not a single sourced dataset — always use the specific product's TDS.
- The Adkwik summary of EN 204 renders the shear-strength requirements ambiguously ("less than 10 N/mm²"); the values given here are stated as *minimum* requirements, which is the correct sense of EN 204, but should be confirmed against the standard itself.

- Stewarts & Lloyds Namibia and Steeledale Windhoek are recorded from third-party directories only; branch addresses, current trading status and product ranges are **unverified**.

- Macsteel's Namibian presence, if any, was not confirmed; the Macsteel branch locator was not successfully read in this pass.

### [`07_materials_and_suppliers/05_timber-and-boards.md`](../07_materials_and_suppliers/05_timber-and-boards.md) — Timber, treatment classes and board products

- **Standard timber lengths** (the 1,8–6,6 m in 300 mm increments convention) were **not verified from a primary source** in this pass; confirm with the supplying yard.
- The MDF, particleboard and plywood thickness ranges in the board table are from general trade practice, **not from a verified manufacturer datasheet**. Confirm against PG Bison and Sonae Arauco current catalogues.
- No Namibian timber treatment plant was identified. Whether any H-class treatment capacity exists in Namibia, or whether all treated timber is imported, is unverified.
- Pennypinchers Timbercity Windhoek is recorded from a third-party directory only.

### [`07_materials_and_suppliers/06_roofing-and-cladding.md`](../07_materials_and_suppliers/06_roofing-and-cladding.md) — Roofing and cladding — profiles, coatings, spans and manufacturers

- **No Namibian roll-former or roofing distributor was verified.** Safintra states SADC affiliates but the specific Namibian entity, its branches and its stock profiles were not confirmed. This is a significant gap for a Namibian project — establish it directly.
- Global Roofing Solutions and Macsteel Roofing branch coverage in Namibia is unverified.
- Detailed span tables for GRS Klip-Lok 700 and for the Fluteline and Trimflute profiles were not extracted; the Safintra tables reproduced here are profile-specific and must not be applied to another manufacturer's product.
- Thatch performance figures (R-value, fire behaviour, required pitch) are described qualitatively; no southern African thatching standard or Agrément certificate was verified in this pass.

### [`07_materials_and_suppliers/07_insulation-membranes-and-sealants.md`](../07_materials_and_suppliers/07_insulation-membranes-and-sealants.md) — Insulation, membranes, DPC/DPM and sealants

- **The Aerolite and Isotherm R-value tables come from an installer's website, not from the manufacturers' own datasheets.** They are internally consistent and match published trade practice, but should be re-verified against Isover/Knauf and Isotherm primary datasheets before being used in a compliance calculation.
- **Lambdaboard R-values per thickness were not retrieved** and are therefore not tabulated. IsoBoard's specific R-value-by-thickness table was also not extracted.
- The SANS 204 climatic zone assignments shown ("zones 1 & 4 & 6" etc.) are as published by the installer and relate to **South African** climatic zones. **Namibia is not covered by SANS 204 zoning** — the applicable Namibian requirement, if any, needs separate verification.
- The sealant joint width/depth rules given are general good practice; they are **not quoted from ISO 11600** (which classifies products, not joint design) and should be checked against the specific manufacturer's data sheet.
- Den Braven's and Alcolin's Namibian distribution was not verified.

### [`07_materials_and_suppliers/08_finishes-and-chemicals.md`](../07_materials_and_suppliers/08_finishes-and-chemicals.md) — Plaster, screeds, tile adhesives, paints and construction chemicals

- The tile adhesive **C/D/R and 1/2/F/T/E/S1/S2 code definitions** are reconstructed from the TAL data sheet's usage plus general EN 12004 practice; the **specific SANS number** adopting EN 12004 was not verified in this pass and is therefore not cited.
- Mapei's, Weber's and Ceresit's southern African product ranges and Namibian availability were **not verified from primary sources**.
- Duram's and Prominent Paints' current ownership was **not verified**.
- The primer/substrate table and the coating-type table are general trade practice, not quoted from a manufacturer specification. Confirm against the chosen manufacturer's system specification.
- No Namibian paint manufacturer was identified; all paint appears to be imported from South Africa. Unverified.

### [`07_materials_and_suppliers/09_suppliers-namibia.md`](../07_materials_and_suppliers/09_suppliers-namibia.md) — Namibian building materials supply landscape

- **All Build it Namibian telephone numbers on buildit.co.za are corrupted** by a formatting error. Real numbers must be obtained per store.
- **The complete Build it Namibia store list (23 stores) has not been enumerated.** Twelve are identified here; the balance are unknown.
- **Agrimark Namibia's branch list is unverified** (site down on the access date).
- **BUCO's Namibian branch network beyond Windhoek is unverified**, as is whether BUCO's roof truss manufacturing operates in Namibia or only in South Africa.
- **Jumbo Charhan could not be verified at all.**
- **No delivery radius, delivery charge, minimum order or lead time was published by any Namibian merchant.** These are the numbers a project actually needs and they must be gathered by telephone.
- No Namibian architectural glass processor, block/brick yard, ready-mix plant, roofing roll-former or timber treatment plant was identified.
- Whether Pupkewitz Megabuild's "19 branches" figure predates recent openings (a 2026 news item refers to Megabuild coming to Gobabis) is unclear — the branch locator lists more than 19 outlets including the specialist stores.

### [`07_materials_and_suppliers/10_suppliers-south-africa.md`](../07_materials_and_suppliers/10_suppliers-south-africa.md) — South African suppliers and cross-border purchasing

- **Häfele South Africa's branch list is unverified** — their locations page returned a security block. Confirm branches directly.
- **Hettich South Africa's and Vepac's local presence and branch structure were not verified** from company-owned pages.
- **Massbuild / Builders' current corporate ownership** following the Massmart/Shoprite transactions was not verified in this pass.
- **The cross-border documentation list is general trade practice**, drawn partly from a source that discloses AI generation; it has **not** been verified against NamRA or SARS primary guidance and must be confirmed with a clearing agent.
- **No prices are quoted in this file.** Online catalogue prices from Builders, Chamberlains and Leroy Merlin change continuously and are SA-inclusive-of-SA-VAT; they are a benchmark, not a quotation.
- Whether a Namibian buyer can practically obtain SA zero-rating on a counter purchase (as opposed to a documented export) was not verified and is a question for the specific supplier.

### [`07_materials_and_suppliers/11_procurement-and-pricing.md`](../07_materials_and_suppliers/11_procurement-and-pricing.md) — Procurement, pricing and quote comparison

- **The lead-time table is a planning estimate, not sourced data.** No Namibian or South African supplier consulted in this research published lead times. Every figure must be replaced with a supplier commitment before it is used to build a programme.
- **No current prices are quoted anywhere in this domain.** The only dated price reference found was Steel Force Namibia's price list dated 08.01.2024, which is described by the supplier as a negotiable guideline and was not retrieved.
- **The South African compulsory VAT registration threshold (R1 million) is stated from general knowledge and was not verified** against SARS in this pass.
- **Discount structures and typical trade discount percentages are not published by any merchant** and are described qualitatively only.
- **Delivery radius, delivery charge and minimum order value for the northern Namibian branches are unknown** and are the single most important commercial fact a project in Ohangwena Region needs to establish.
- Whether Namibian merchants will hold a project rate against steel and cement price movement, and for how long, is unverified.

### [`08_glass_and_facades/00_overview.md`](../08_glass_and_facades/00_overview.md) — Glass and facades — domain overview

- The exact clause numbering of SANS 10400-N:2012 has not been read from the standard itself; the requirements summarised here come from published commentary. Treat clause citations as indicative until the purchased standard is checked.
- Namibia's adoption status of the SANS suite for glazing is not verified — see `05_standards-and-safety.md`.
- Latitude-specific solar geometry above is computed from the standard solar-noon relation, not taken from a published table; verify against site-specific solar data before using it for a shading design.

### [`08_glass_and_facades/01_glass-types-and-manufacture.md`](../08_glass_and_facades/01_glass-types-and-manufacture.md) — Glass types and manufacture

- PFG publishes iron oxide content as **0.075%** on its "All about glass" page; a second rendering of the same table gives **0.75%**. The lower figure is consistent with ordinary clear float; treat 0.075% as the likely correct value but verify with PFG technical services.
- **EN 12150-1 minimum fragment counts per 50 × 50 mm by thickness were not obtained from a primary source** and are deliberately not stated here. Read the standard before quoting them.
- Toughening furnace temperatures (620–650 °C) are given as the industry range and were not verified from a manufacturer document in this research pass.
- PFG's current thickness and size availability may have changed since the 2016 brochure; confirm with PFG or PG Building Glass before specifying.

### [`08_glass_and_facades/02_performance-and-coatings.md`](../08_glass_and_facades/02_performance-and-coatings.md) — Glass performance and coatings

- The PFG performance tables date from the October 2016 brochure. Product availability, coating densities and data may have changed; request current data sheets from PFG technical services before specifying.
- **No verified southern African data was obtained for double-glazed (IGU) make-ups using PFG coated products.** The Rw table includes IGUs, but SHGC/U-value data for IGU make-ups is not reproduced here because it was not sourced. Do not extrapolate single-glazed centre-of-glass values to IGUs.
- Emissivity values for specific pyrolytic and sputtered coatings were not obtained from manufacturer literature; the 0.1–0.2 / 0.03–0.05 ranges given are indicative industry figures and should be verified per product.
- Coating surface conventions (#1–#4) are standard practice but were not verified from a cited document in this pass.
- Self-cleaning coating performance in a high-dust, low-rainfall environment is asserted from first principles, not from tested data.

### [`08_glass_and_facades/03_structural-glass-and-facades.md`](../08_glass_and_facades/03_structural-glass-and-facades.md) — Structural glass and facade engineering

- **Deflection limits of L/240, L/500 and 19 mm** are stated as widely used industry practice; only **L/175** was verified from a cited source. Confirm against EN 13830, the system supplier's technical manual and the project specification.
- SANS 10160 wind loading clause references have not been verified from the standard.
- The SANS 10400-N deemed-to-satisfy thickness tables reproduced in section 3 come from published commentary, not from the standard itself.
- No southern African facade-engineering practice guide was located in this research pass; the wind zoning and pressure-multiplier statements are general engineering practice, not cited.
- Minimum edge distances and hole diameters for point fixings are manufacturer-specific and were not verified.

### [`08_glass_and_facades/04_glazing-installation-and-detailing.md`](../08_glass_and_facades/04_glazing-installation-and-detailing.md) — Glazing installation and detailing

- **The specific dimensional requirements of SANS 10137 (edge clearance, edge cover, setting block sizes and positions) have not been read from the standard.** The quarter-point setting-block rule, the 2:1 sealant width-to-depth ratio, the 1:10 sill fall, gasket Shore hardness and vacuum-lifter safety factors given above are standard trade practice, not cited requirements. Verify against SANS 10137 and the system supplier's manual before writing them into a specification.
- Glass weights per m² are calculated from a nominal density of 2 500 kg/m³ and are approximate.
- Crealco system designations were taken from the company website in August 2026 and change with product releases.
- Namibian practice on putty, steel windows and site glazing method has not been separately verified.

### [`08_glass_and_facades/05_standards-and-safety.md`](../08_glass_and_facades/05_standards-and-safety.md) — Glass standards and safety requirements

- **SANS 613 has not been verified.** Its full title, current edition and scope are not confirmed. It is referenced from SANS 10400-N in relation to impact and wind loads on glazing, per secondary commentary. Confirm before citing.
- **SANS 10137's full title and current edition are unverified**, as are its dimensional requirements for edge clearance, edge cover and setting blocks.
- **No SANS 10400-N clause numbers are cited anywhere in this domain**, deliberately. The commentary sources do not give them.
- **EN 12600 drop heights and breakage-mode letters are deliberately omitted** because no primary source was obtained. The classification structure α(β)φ is described but its values are not.
- **EN 12150-1 minimum fragment counts by thickness were not obtained** and are omitted.
- **SANS 1263 coverage of safety film** (which part, what classification, what marking) is unverified — the brief's reference to SANS 1263 for safety film could not be confirmed against a primary source.
- **Namibia's legal adoption of any glazing standard is unverified.**
- **The SANS 204 fenestration calculation method is unverified** beyond the 15% deemed-to-satisfy threshold.
- The two commentary sources on critical locations disagree in detail; the standard governs.

### [`08_glass_and_facades/06_landmark-glass-projects.md`](../08_glass_and_facades/06_landmark-glass-projects.md) — Landmark glass projects

- **Discovery Place Sandton, Sheikh Zayed Desert Learning Centre and Zumthor's Gugalun House are unverified** and are described above only as gaps.
- **The Leonardo's facade contractor, facade engineer, glass supplier and glazing make-up are unverified.**
- **Portside Tower's curtain wall system, glass make-up and facade contractor are unverified.**
- The Willis Tower 2014 incident is described in the source as damage to "the laminated glass flooring"; the widely reported attribution of the damage to the **protective coating** rather than the structural glass is stated here as a design lesson but was not confirmed from the cited source.
- The Shard's glass type (whether low-iron / extra-white, and whether single or double glazed) and its facade contractor were not verified.
- The Louvre Pyramid's glass thickness and exact make-up were not verified.
- Apple Fifth Avenue's glass manufacturer was not verified. Josef Gartner and sedak are verified only for the Steve Jobs Theater facade.

- **All Namibian business listings above are from a crowd-sourced directory and are unverified.** Confirm existence, current contact details and actual capability by telephone before relying on any of them.

- **SAGGA (South African Glass and Glazing Association) could not be reached**; its scope, membership and technical publications are unverified.
- **Smart Glass and Independent Glass** as named companies could not be verified.
- **Consol** as a container-glass-only producer is stated from trade knowledge; its website was unreachable.
- **Hulamin's** current divisions and products are unverified.
- **Schüco SA, AluK SA, Reynaers and Technal** southern African presence, offices and licensed fabricators are unverified.

### [`08_glass_and_facades/08_specifying-glass.md`](../08_glass_and_facades/08_specifying-glass.md) — Specifying glass and glazing

- **All lead times are indicative trade practice and are not sourced.** Confirm with the named processor for every project.
- **All warranty periods are left as bracketed placeholders** because no manufacturer warranty documents were obtained in this research pass. PFG publishes a warranty for its copper-free mirrors; it was not retrieved.
- **SANS 10137's dimensional requirements** (setting-block positions and sizes, edge clearance, edge cover) are not verified; clause 4.2 of the template reflects trade practice and must be reconciled with the standard.
- **Visual quality acceptance criteria** (permitted defect sizes and densities by viewing distance) are in the EN product standards but were not retrieved; do not write numbers into a specification without reading them.
- **[NA]** The Namibian regulatory reference in clause 1.2 is deliberately conditional because Namibia's adoption of SANS 10400-N is unverified — see `05_standards-and-safety.md`.

### [`09_equipment_manufacturers/09_manual-and-documentation-library.md`](../09_equipment_manufacturers/09_manual-and-documentation-library.md) — Manual and documentation library — tested link register for construction equipment

- **Grundfos**: neither `grundfos.com` nor `product-selection.grundfos.com` was reachable from this environment (DNS/proxy level, not a 403). The Grundfos Product Center is the industry-standard pump selection and documentation tool and should be opened directly. Status `needs-verification`.
- **Caterpillar**: cat.com, parts.cat.com and safety.cat.com all return 403 to automated clients. The URLs are correct and current but could not be status-verified here.
- **PERI**: the group-level `/en/downloads.html` path now serves a "content has moved" notice; downloads have been devolved to country sites. Use `peri.co.za` for **[ZA] [NA]**.
- **Doka**: no stable public downloads URL was found at group level; user information (instructions for assembly and use) is reached from each system's product page. Worth re-checking.
- **JCB**: JCB does not publish a general public operator-manual library; literature is dealer-routed. `jcb.com/en-gb/support` is the correct entry point.
- Several sites are single-page applications that return HTTP 200 for unknown paths. Where a URL below is used programmatically, content-check the response, do not trust the status code alone.

- **[NA]** Namibian operator certification and machinery inspection requirements were not verified against the Namibian Labour Act regulations; confirm with the Namibian Ministry of Labour and with the project insurer.
- **[ZA]** TETA/CETA accreditation status of any specific training provider must be checked directly; accreditations lapse.

### [`14_software_blender/05_lighting-and-rendering.md`](../14_software_blender/05_lighting-and-rendering.md) — Lighting, rendering and output in Blender

- **[NA]** Okongo's coordinates are given to one decimal place as supplied in the brief (≈17.4° S, 17.6° E). Take the exact site coordinates from the survey diagram before a shading study is used to justify an overhang depth — a 0.1° error is ~11 km and shifts solar time by ~24 s, which is negligible, but a sign or transposition error is not.
- Namibia's permanent UTC+2 (no DST) is stated from general knowledge of the 2017 change and is **needs-verification** against the current statute if legal precision is required.
- The lumens→radiant-watts conversion factor (~300 lm/W for white LED) is a working approximation, not a Blender-documented figure; Blender provides no lumens input.
- The clear-sky sun value of ~1000 W/m² is the standard solar-constant-at-sea-level figure, not a Blender default; the Blender default Sun strength is much lower.
- Whether `Filmic` remains selectable in 5.2 or has been removed: the manual still documents it and marks it deprecated.

### [`14_software_blender/06_python-api-and-automation.md`](../14_software_blender/06_python-api-and-automation.md) — The Blender Python API and automation

- Modifier property identifiers used above were confirmed against the API reference: `SolidifyModifier.solidify_mode` is `'EXTRUDE'` (Simple) / `'NON_MANIFOLD'` (Complex); `BooleanModifier.solver` is `'FLOAT'` / `'EXACT'` / `'MANIFOLD'` and `material_mode` is `'INDEX'` / `'TRANSFER'`; `BevelModifier.limit_method` is `'NONE'` / `'ANGLE'` / `'WEIGHT'` / `'VGROUP'`.
- `cycles.adaptive_min_samples`, `cycles.denoising_input_passes` and `cycles.blur_glossy` property names are used from working knowledge; the Cycles add-on's properties are not in the core RNA docs. Verify against `bpy.context.scene.cycles.bl_rna.properties` on the target build.
- `scene.view_settings.look = 'AgX - Base Contrast'` — the exact Look string depends on the active OCIO config; enumerate `bpy.types.ColorManagedViewSettings.bl_rna.properties['look'].enum_items` rather than hard-coding.
- The render-engine identifier passed to `scene.render.engine` (`'BLENDER_EEVEE_NEXT'` vs `'BLENDER_EEVEE'`) differs between the 4.2–4.5 and 5.x series; resolve it at runtime.

### [`16_walls_and_boundaries/03_boundary-wall-design-and-engineering.md`](../16_walls_and_boundaries/03_boundary-wall-design-and-engineering.md) — Boundary wall design and engineering

- **Design wind pressure.** The 0,70 kN/m² used in the worked examples is an assumption. Read the fundamental basic wind speed for Ohangwena from **SANS 10160-3 Annex A** and derive `q_p` and `c_f` properly, including the end-zone coefficients. `needs-verification`
- **Permissible flexural tensile stress** for masonry normal to bed joints with Class II mortar in the SANS framework was not obtained from a primary source; 0,10 MPa is used as a conservative working value. `needs-verification`
- **SANS 10400-H foundation tables** were not machine-readable in this pass. The foundation schedule here is derived from first principles and should be checked against Part H. `needs-verification`
- Table 19's clay/reinforced row (16 → 6 m) reads anomalously in the transcribed text; verify against a clean copy of the standard.
- Watermeyer's *Free-standing walls — a design guide* (CMA, 1993) should be obtained and this file reconciled with it.

- The §2 vertical-surface irradiation table is computed with an **isotropic sky model and a ground albedo of 0.30**. Pale bare Kalahari sand may have an albedo of 0.35–0.45, which would raise all vertical-surface figures; the *relative ranking between orientations* is robust, the absolute values are indicative.
- All Okongo figures derive from reanalysis or from the nearest stations (Eenhana ~120 km, Ondangwa ~180 km). There is no station record at Okongo.

- Southern African bed and door sizes should be confirmed against current local manufacturer schedules.

### [`21_machine_vision/09_practical-projects.md`](../21_machine_vision/09_practical-projects.md) — Four worked project blueprints — from site photos to a joinery panel gauge

### [`33_social_engineering_defence/01_the-psychology.md`](../33_social_engineering_defence/01_the-psychology.md) — The psychology of influence — why social engineering works

- **Prospect theory and the affect heuristic** are cited here from general knowledge of Kahneman & Tversky (1979) and Slovic et al.; the primary papers were not fetched during construction. The claims made are standard textbook content, but the specific loss-aversion coefficient (~2×) should be checked against the original before being quoted precisely. Marked `needs-verification` at the level of the numeric coefficient only.
- **Danziger et al. (2011) parole study** and the **ego-depletion replication failures** (Hagger et al. multi-lab RRR) are described from general knowledge; primary sources not fetched. The contested status is well established but citations should be added.
- **Pronin's bias blind spot** work and the "intelligence trap" literature are cited from general knowledge; primary sources not fetched.
- Cialdini's *Pre-Suasion* unity principle is verified; the experimental basis for unity specifically is thinner than for the original six and should be characterised cautiously.

### [`35_health_and_fitness/01_exercise-physiology.md`](../35_health_and_fitness/01_exercise-physiology.md) — Exercise physiology — muscle, energy systems, adaptation and fatigue

- Sarcomere optimal-length figures and typical fibre-type percentages are drawn from standard textbook physiology (McArdle/Katch; NSCA Essentials) rather than from a single retrievable primary source, and the individual ranges are very wide. Treat the tabulated values as representative, not definitive.
- PCr resynthesis half-time (~20–30 s) and the ~3–5 min near-full-restoration figure are textbook consensus values from ³¹P-MRS studies; they vary with fibre type, training status and muscle oxygenation. `needs-verification` against a primary MRS source.
- The relative contribution of central versus peripheral mechanisms to fatigue in resistance exercise specifically (as opposed to endurance exercise) remains poorly quantified.

### [`35_health_and_fitness/02_hypertrophy-and-strength-science.md`](../35_health_and_fitness/02_hypertrophy-and-strength-science.md) — Hypertrophy and strength science — what the evidence actually says

- The rate-of-gain table is a **practitioner heuristic** (Aragon), not a meta-analytic result. It is widely used and broadly consistent with trial data but has no single primary citation. Marked `needs-verification` as a quantitative claim.
- The upper end of the volume dose-response (above ~20 sets/muscle/week) is genuinely unresolved; individual trials disagree and sample sizes are small.
- The "effective reps" model is a mechanistically motivated heuristic with only partial empirical support; the specific claim that ~5 reps before failure are the stimulating ones is not directly measured.
- Whether 3× weekly frequency exceeds 2× on a volume-equated basis remains undetermined (explicitly stated by Schoenfeld 2016).
- Lengthened-partials effect size remains contested; the largest trial (Gschneidner 2025) is null while several smaller trials are positive.

### [`35_health_and_fitness/03_programme-design.md`](../35_health_and_fitness/03_programme-design.md) — Programme design — from needs analysis to worked programmes

- The **volume landmark table** (minimum effective dose / productive range) is a practitioner framework, not a meta-analytic output. The per-muscle numbers are convention and are marked `needs-verification` as quantitative claims.
- **Deload frequency and structure** have almost no direct experimental evidence. All recommendations here are reasoned from fitness–fatigue theory and coaching practice.
- Whether **VBT-guided training** outperforms well-executed RPE-guided training in matched conditions is not established.
- The magnitude of the **interference effect** at realistic recreational cardio volumes is smaller than commonly claimed but has not been well quantified in trained lifters.

### [`35_health_and_fitness/04_technique-and-the-main-lifts.md`](../35_health_and_fitness/04_technique-and-the-main-lifts.md) — Technique and the main lifts — mechanics, cues, faults and injury risk

- Specific EMG comparisons cited (chin-up versus pull-up biceps contribution; hip thrust gluteal activation) are described qualitatively because the primary EMG papers were not directly retrieved in this pass. Marked `needs-verification`.
- The claim that deep squatting does not increase knee injury risk in trained populations is well-supported in narrative reviews but was not verified here against a specific systematic review. `needs-verification`.
- The overhead-press shoulder-flexion range figure (~170–180°) is a textbook value, not a cited measurement.
- Direct hypertrophy comparisons of hip thrust versus squat for gluteus maximus are limited; conclusions in the popular literature outrun the trial evidence.

- The **leucine threshold** figure of ~2–3 g per meal is widely cited but the ISSN stand gives a much wider range (700–3000 mg). The precise threshold, and whether it is a threshold at all rather than a continuum, is not settled.
- **Micronutrient risk table** entries are drawn from general nutrition consensus rather than from individually cited primary sources; treat as orientation. `needs-verification` on specific deficiency prevalence figures.
- **DIAAS values** for specific proteins and the "10–20% more protein for plant-based eaters" adjustment are practitioner heuristics rather than meta-analytic outputs.

- **Regional micronutrient status data for Namibia** specifically were not located. `needs-verification`.

### [`35_health_and_fitness/06_body-composition-management.md`](../35_health_and_fitness/06_body-composition-management.md) — Body composition management — gaining, losing, measuring, and the risks at both extremes

- **Measurement error figures** in section 7 are synthesised from method-validation literature generally rather than from a single retrieved systematic comparison. Marked `needs-verification` as specific numbers; the relative ordering of methods is well established.
- **Body fat percentage category boundaries** vary between sources (ACE, ACSM, Jackson-Pollock norms) and are convention rather than consensus. `needs-verification`.
- The **32 MJ (~7 700 kcal) per kg of body fat** figure is a classical approximation that ignores the composition of tissue lost and adaptive changes; dynamic models (e.g. the NIH Body Weight Planner) are more accurate.
- The **refeed** literature in trained populations is a single small RCT plus a published reanalysis dispute. Effect size and mechanism remain uncertain.
- **REDs prevalence data** by sport and by sex were not extracted in this pass; see the 2023 IOC statement directly.

- **Vitamin D and athletic performance** meta-analyses were not directly retrieved; the "corrects deficiency only" position is drawn from the general pattern of the micronutrient literature. `needs-verification`.

More recent surveys exist but were not retrieved; the rate today may differ, though periodic analytical studies continue to find substantial adulteration, particularly in weight-loss and pre-workout categories. `needs-verification` for current figures.

### [`35_health_and_fitness/08_performance-enhancing-drugs.md`](../35_health_and_fitness/08_performance-enhancing-drugs.md) — Performance-enhancing drugs — pharmacology, harms, law and anti-doping

- The **hazard ratio for mortality** in Windfeld-Mathiasen et al. (JAMA 2024) is not stated here because the research letter's abstract was not retrievable through the sources available in this pass. The study exists and is correctly cited; the specific effect size is `needs-verification`.
- The **WADA Prohibited List category structure** (S0–S9, M1–M3, P1) is stated from established knowledge. WADA's own site returned a robots-disallowed error and a Wikipedia summary misassigned several categories between the "at all times" and "in-competition" groups. **The list is revised annually — verify against the current year's official PDF at wada-ama.org before relying on any specific classification.** `needs-verification`.
- **South African** schedule designation for specific anabolic agents under Act 101 of 1965, and the current SAIDS enabling and amendment acts, could not be verified against primary sources (the SAIDS website returned a server error). **[ZA]** `needs-verification`.
- **Namibian** schedule designations under Act 13 of 2003 and the current national anti-doping arrangements could not be verified against primary sources. **[NA]** `needs-verification`.
- **EU member state variation** is described qualitatively; a state-by-state table was not compiled.
- The claim that unmonitored EPO use explains the cluster of cyclist deaths circa 1987–1991 is **widely reported but epidemiologically unproven**; it is presented here as attribution, not established fact.
- Reported **insulin and diuretic fatalities in bodybuilders** are drawn from case-report and forensic literature that was not individually retrieved in this pass. `needs-verification` for specific counts.

### [`35_health_and_fitness/09_recovery-sleep-and-health.md`](../35_health_and_fitness/09_recovery-sleep-and-health.md) — Recovery, sleep and health — what works between sessions

- The **sleep hygiene table** synthesises evidence quality from the general sleep-medicine literature rather than from individually retrieved meta-analyses for each row. `needs-verification` on the specific gradings.
- Whether **post-exercise heat exposure** interferes with hypertrophy adaptation the way cold does is not well studied; the current position (that it does not) is provisional.
- **Whole-body cryotherapy** versus cold water immersion has not been directly compared for adaptation-blunting effects at adequate scale.
- **Sleep requirement for athletes in heavy training** is often quoted as 9–10 h; the evidence for a specific elevated requirement is weaker than the evidence that more sleep helps performance in already-restricted athletes.
- The **Mah 2011** sleep-extension study had n = 11 and no control group; the effect magnitudes should be treated as provisional despite the strong p-values.
- Whether the **Laukkanen sauna** association is causal cannot be determined from an observational cohort; reverse causation (healthier men sauna more) is a live alternative explanation.

### [`35_health_and_fitness/10_special-populations-and-longevity.md`](../35_health_and_fitness/10_special-populations-and-longevity.md) — Special populations and longevity — training across contexts

- The **specific recommendations of the 2019 Canadian pregnancy guideline** are summarised from the widely cited version; the abstract retrieved describes the methodology rather than enumerating the recommendations. **Verify against the full guideline before clinical use.** `needs-verification`.
- **Chronic low back pain** claims (imaging–pain dissociation prevalence figures, comparative modality evidence) are drawn from the general guideline literature; no specific systematic review was retrieved in this pass. `needs-verification`.
- The **VO2max decline per 1 000 m of altitude** (~6–8%) is a textbook figure with wide individual variation; no primary source was verified.
- **Sarcopenia onset age and rate of loss** figures are consensus values from the geriatrics literature rather than a retrieved primary source.
- **Blue Zones critique** references analyses of birth-record and pension-fraud confounding that were not retrieved in this pass; the criticism is real and published but the specific citation is `needs-verification`.
- **Protein requirements for older adults (1.2–1.6 g/kg/day)** derive from the PROT-AGE and ESPEN expert group recommendations, not retrieved directly here. `needs-verification`.
- No **Namibia-specific** environmental physiology literature was located; the regional notes are inferred from general climate characteristics and heat/altitude physiology. **[NA]** `needs-verification`.
